Request parsing (JSON deserialization)\n  2. Filter validation\n  3. JavaScript engine initialization\n  4. FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES\n```java\nObjectMapper mapper = new ObjectMapper();\nmapper.configure(DeserializationFeature.FAIL_ON_UNKNOWN_PROPERTIES, true);\n```\nThis prevents unknown properties from being silently ignored.\n\n### 2. Custom Validation Annotations\n```java\n@JsonAnySetter\npublic void handleUnknownProperty(String key, Object value) {\n    if (!key.isEmpty() && !ALLOWED_PROPERTIES.contains(key)) {\n        throw new JsonMappingException(\"Unknown property: \" + key);\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Immutable Data Classes\n- Use `@JsonCreator` for controlled deserialization\n- Define all allowed properties explicitly\n- No setters for properties not expected from JSON\n\n## Implementation Pattern\n```java\npublic class JavaScriptFilter {\n    private final String function;\n    private final Map<String, Object> context;\n\n    @JsonCreator\n    public JavaScriptFilter(\n        @JsonProperty(\"function\") String function,\n        @JsonProperty(\"type\") String type) {\n        // Only accept expected properties\n        this.function = function;\n    }\n}\n```\n\n## Validation Best Practices\n1. **Whitelist Approach**: Only accept known properties\n2. **Type Checking**: Ensure properties have expected types\n3. **Early Validation**: Validate on deserialization, not execution\n4. **Immutability**: Make configuration objects immutable after creation\n\n## Testing Deserialization Security\n```java\n// Should throw exception\nmapper.readValue(\"{\\\"function\\\": \\\"...\\\", \\\"\\\": {\\\"enabled\\\": true}}\",\n                 JavaScriptFilter.class);\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":603,"cache_read_input_tokens":19337,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":603,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5b683495-287e-4017-8314-a3e2bb208752","uuid":"c0769b21-3767-4433-8c47-4290fb8921af"}

Classloader Restrictions\n```java\n// Prevent access to dangerous Java classes\nClassFilter classFilter = new RestrictedClassFilter();\nengine.setClassFilter(classFilter);\n```\n\n### 2. Execution Context\n```java\n// Wrap execution with security manager\ntry (AccessController context = new RestrictedContext()) {\n    engine.eval(jsFunction);\n}\n```\n\n### 3. Function Sandboxing\n```javascript\n// Validate function syntax before execution\nfunction validateAndExecute(fn) {\n    if (containsDangerousPatterns(fn)) {\n        throw new SecurityException(\"Dangerous pattern detected\");\n    }\n    return executeInRestrictedContext(fn);\n}\n```\n\n## Validation Checks\nBefore executing any user-provided JavaScript:\n1. Parse and validate syntax\n2. Static analysis for dangerous calls (java.lang.*, java.io.*, etc.)\n3. Runtime security manager enforcement\n4. Capability-based execution model (whitelist safe operations)\n\n## Testing Security\n```java\n// Test that dangerous operations are blocked\nString maliciousCode = \"java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec('ls')\";\nassertThrows(SecurityException.class,\n    () -> engine.eval(maliciousCode));\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. **Whitelist over Blacklist**: Allow only safe operations\n2. **Static Analysis**: Check code before execution\n3. **Multiple Layers**: Don't rely on single security mechanism\n4. **Logging**: Log all JavaScript execution attempts\n5. **Disable Dangerous APIs**: Turn off access to java.lang, java.io, etc.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":622,"cache_read_input_tokens":19940,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":622,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5b683495-287e-4017-8314-a3e2bb208752","uuid":"9b8f0748-0ff6-4f76-902e-3f5fc50bcdb0"}

Clear Naming\n- `0001-fix-javascript-filter-security.patch`\n- `0002-add-filter-validation.patch`\n\n### 2. Patch Content\n- Include minimal context (3 lines before/after)\n- Update only necessary code\n- Include comments explaining the fix\n\n### 3. Testing Patches\n```bash\n# Reset to original state\ngit reset --hard HEAD\n\n# Apply patch\ngit apply < patch.patch\n\n# Verify changes\ngit diff HEAD\n```\n\n### 4. Multiple Patches\nNumber patches sequentially for application order:\n1. `0001-base-fix.patch`\n2. `0002-validation.patch`\n3. `0003-testing.patch`\n\n## Patch Anatomy\n- Header with file paths\n- Line numbers and context size (`@@ -10,5 +10,5 @@`)\n- Unchanged lines (space prefix)\n- Removed lines (`-` prefix)\n- Added lines (`+` prefix)\n\n## Common Issues\n\n### Whitespace Problems\n```bash\n# Use --ignore-whitespace to be lenient\ngit apply --ignore-whitespace patch.patch\n```\n\n### Line Ending Issues\n```bash\n# Convert line endings\ndos2unix file.java\n```\n\n### Offset Detection\nGit can handle minor offsets if context is sufficient.\n\n## Verification\n```bash\n# Ensure patch applied cleanly\ngit status  # Should show modified files\ngit diff    # Should show expected changes\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":649,"cache_read_input_tokens":20562,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":649,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5b683495-287e-4017-8314-a3e2bb208752","uuid":"e029ea4c-aed8-4555-b616-1dbe9d2278dd"}

Clear Naming\n- `0001-fix-javascript-filter-security.patch`\n- `0002-add-filter-validation.patch`\n\n### 2. Patch Content\n- Include minimal context (3 lines before/after)\n- Update only necessary code\n- Include comments explaining the fix\n\n### 3. Testing Patches\n```bash\n# Reset to original state\ngit reset --hard HEAD\n\n# Apply patch\ngit apply < patch.patch\n\n# Verify changes\ngit diff HEAD\n```\n\n### 4. Multiple Patches\nNumber patches sequentially for application order:\n1. `0001-base-fix.patch`\n2. `0002-validation.patch`\n3. `0003-testing.patch`\n\n## Patch Anatomy\n- Header with file paths\n- Line numbers and context size (`@@ -10,5 +10,5 @@`)\n- Unchanged lines (space prefix)\n- Removed lines (`-` prefix)\n- Added lines (`+` prefix)\n\n## Common Issues\n\n### Whitespace Problems\n```bash\n# Use --ignore-whitespace to be lenient\ngit apply --ignore-whitespace patch.patch\n```\n\n### Line Ending Issues\n```bash\n# Convert line endings\ndos2unix file.java\n```\n\n### Offset Detection\nGit can handle minor offsets if context is sufficient.\n\n## Verification\n```bash\n# Ensure patch applied cleanly\ngit status  # Should show modified files\ngit diff    # Should show expected changes\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Transforms\n    32→ * that shadow fields may still refer to the fields they shadow. This can be used to transform a field \"in-place\".\n    33→ *\n    34→ * Transforms do have some limitations. See the License for the\n    22→  ~ specific language governing permissions and limitations\n    23→  ~ under the License.\n    24→  -->\n    25→\n    26→\n    27→This page discusses how to use JavaScript to extend Apache Druid.\n    28→\n    29→## Examples\n    30→\n    31→JavaScript can be used to extend Druid in a variety of ways:\n    32→\n    33→- [Aggregators](../querying/aggregations.html#javascript-aggregator)\n    34→- [Extraction functions](../querying/dimensionspecs.html#javascript-extraction-function)\n    35→- [Filters](../querying/filters.html#javascript-filter)\n    36→- [Post-aggregators](../querying/post-aggregations.html#javascript-post-aggregator)\n    37→- [Input parsers](../ingestion/data-formats.html#javascript-parsespec)\n    38→- [Router strategy](../design/router.html#javascript)\n    39→- [Worker select strategy](../configuration/index.html#javascript-worker-select-strategy)\n    40→\n    41→JavaScript can be injected dynamically at runtime, making it convenient to rapidly prototype new functionality\n    42→without needing to write and deploy Druid extensions.\n    43→\n    44→Druid uses the Mozilla Rhino engine at optimization level 9 to compile and execute JavaScript.\n    45→\n    46→## Security\n    47→\n    48→Druid does not execute JavaScript functions in a sandbox, so they have full access to the machine. So JavaScript\n    49→functions allow users to execute arbitrary code inside druid process. So, by default, JavaScript is disabled.\n    50→However, on dev/staging environments or secured production environments you can enable those by setting\n    51→the [configuration property](../configuration/index.html#javascript)\n    52→`druid.javascript.enabled = true`.\n    53→\n    54→## Global variables\n    55→\n    56→Avoid using global variables. Druid may share the global scope between multiple threads, which can lead to\n    57→unpredictable results if global variables are used.\n    58→\n    59→## Performance\n    60→\n    61→Simple JavaScript functions typically have a slight performance penalty to native speed. More complex JavaScript\n    62→functions can have steeper performance penalties. Druid compiles JavaScript functions once on each data process per query.\n    63→\n    64→You may need to pay special attention to garbage collection when making heavy use of JavaScript functions, especially\n    65→garbage collection of the compiled classes themselves. Be sure to use a garbage collector configuration that supports\n    66→timely collection of unused classes (this is generally easier on JDK8 with the Metaspace than it is on JDK7).\n    67→\n    68→## JavaScript vs. Native Extensions\n    69→\n    70→Generally we recommend using JavaScript when security is not an issue, and when speed of development is more important\n    71→than performance or memory use. If security is an issue, or if performance and memory use are of the utmost importance,\n    72→we recommend developing a native Druid extension.\n    73→\n    74→In addition, native Druid extensions are more flexible than JavaScript functions. There are some kinds of extensions\n    75→(like sketches) that must be written as native Druid extensions due to their need for custom data formats.\n    76→\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. JSON with `\"type\": \"javascript\"` is received\n2. Jackson routes to `JavaScriptDimFilter.class` based on `@JsonSubTypes` mapping\n3. `JavaScriptDimFilter`'s `@JsonCreator` constructor is called\n4. So JavaScript functions allow users to execute arbitrary \ncode inside druid process. So, by default, JavaScript is disabled.\n```\n\n**Key Security Points:**\n1. **NO SANDBOX**: JavaScript functions have full JVM access - this is explicitly documented\n2. **DISABLED BY DEFAULT**: `druid.javascript.enabled` defaults to false\n3. **RUNTIME CHECKING**: JavaScript enablement is checked at execution time, not deserialization time\n4. **MOZILLA RHINO ENGINE**: Uses Rhino at optimization level 9 (lines 237, 333, 45)\n5. **NO ACCESS CONTROLS**: No sandboxing, whitelist filtering, or code analysis\n\n**Mozilla Rhino Context Management:**\nAll JavaScript execution wraps code in Context management:\n```java\nfinal Context cx = Context.enter();\ntry {\n    cx.setOptimizationLevel(9);\n    scope = cx.initStandardObjects();\n    fnApply = cx.compileFunction(scope, script, \"script\", 1, null);\n}\nfinally {\n    Context.exit();\n}\n```\n\nThis pattern appears consistently in:\n- `JavaScriptDimFilter.JavaScriptPredicateFactory` (lines 235-243)\n- `JavaScriptAggregatorFactory.compileScript()` (lines 331-340)\n- `JavaScriptExtractionFn` (lines 43-50)\n- `JavaScriptPostAggregator` (similar pattern)\n\n### 5. Code Path Summary: JSON to Execution\n\n```\nJSON Input with \"type\": \"javascript\"\n        ↓\nJackson Deserializer (DimFilter @JsonSubTypes)\n        ↓\nJavaScriptDimFilter Constructor\n  - Validates dimension and function are not null\n  - Stores function string (NO VALIDATION)\n  - Stores JavaScriptConfig reference\n        ↓\ntoFilter() Method Called\n        ↓\ngetPredicateFactory() (Thread-safe with synchronization)\n  - Checks config.isEnabled() - throws if disabled\n  - Compiles function via Rhino Context.compileFunction()\n  - Returns JavaScriptPredicateFactory\n        ↓\nJavaScriptFilter Execution\n  - makeMatcher() or getBitmapResult() called\n  - Rhino Context.enter() creates execution context\n  - fnApply.call() executes the JavaScript function\n```\n\n### 6. Notable Findings\n\n**No Input Sanitization:**\nThe `function` parameter is accepted as a raw string with no validation, syntax checking, or content analysis before compilation.\n\n**Thread-Safety Patterns:**\nUses volatile fields and synchronized blocks around script compilation to ensure proper publication and avoid recompilation.\n\n**Injection Pattern:**\n`JavaScriptConfig` is injected via `@JacksonInject`, indicating it comes from the Guice dependency injection container configured at application startup.\n\n**Transform vs Filter:**\n- `TransformSpec` handles both filters and data transformations\n- Filters (via `DimFilter`) are polymorphic and can include JavaScript filters\n- Transform objects use `ExpressionTransform` (safer) rather than raw JavaScript\n\nAll key files are in `/root/druid/processing/src/main/java/` and `/root/druid/core/src/main/java/` directories."},{"type":"text","text":"agentId: a2ae25f (for resuming to continue this agent's work if needed)"}]}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5b683495-287e-4017-8314-a3e2bb208752","uuid":"284dbc06-1b16-49b9-9a7f-1752d7b532d3","tool_use_result":{"status":"completed","prompt":"Search the Druid codebase for JavaScript filter implementation. Code Path Summary: JSON to Execution\n\n```\nJSON Input with \"type\": \"javascript\"\n        ↓\nJackson Deserializer (DimFilter @JsonSubTypes)\n        ↓\nJavaScriptDimFilter Constructor\n  - Validates dimension and function are not null\n  - Stores function string (NO VALIDATION)\n  - Stores JavaScriptConfig reference\n        ↓\ntoFilter() Method Called\n        ↓\ngetPredicateFactory() (Thread-safe with synchronization)\n  - Checks config.isEnabled() - throws if disabled\n  - Compiles function via Rhino Context.compileFunction()\n  - Returns JavaScriptPredicateFactory\n        ↓\nJavaScriptFilter Execution\n  - makeMatcher() or getBitmapResult() called\n  - Rhino Context.enter() creates execution context\n  - fnApply.call() executes the JavaScript function\n```\n\n### 6. Notable Findings\n\n**No Input Sanitization:**\nThe `function` parameter is accepted as a raw string with no validation, syntax checking, or content analysis before compilation.\n\n**Thread-Safety Patterns:**\nUses volatile fields and synchronized blocks around script compilation to ensure proper publication and avoid recompilation.\n\n**Injection Pattern:**\n`JavaScriptConfig` is injected via `@JacksonInject`, indicating it comes from the Guice dependency injection container configured at application startup.\n\n**Transform vs Filter:**\n- `TransformSpec` handles both filters and data transformations\n- Filters (via `DimFilter`) are polymorphic and can include JavaScript filters\n- Transform objects use `ExpressionTransform` (safer) rather than raw JavaScript\n\nAll key files are in `/root/druid/processing/src/main/java/` and `/root/druid/core/src/main/java/` directories."}],"totalDurationMs":106092,"totalTokens":59872,"totalToolUseCount":42,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1800,"cache_read_input_tokens":58071,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1800,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01VjacDJ79C5dznVmfvA5GUc","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01NqYvkYDeLWbeGxiSPD4uT2","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/patches/VULNERABILITY_ANALYSIS.md","content":"# Apache Druid 0.20.0 JavaScript Filter Vulnerability Analysis and Fix\n\n## Vulnerability Overview\n\n**CVE**: Remote Code Execution via JavaScript Filter Deserialization\n**Affected Version**: Apache Druid 0.20.0\n**Attack Vector**: Authenticated HTTP POST to `/druid/indexer/v1/sampler` endpoint\n**Severity**: Critical (CVSS 8.0+)\n\n## Technical Details\n\n### Vulnerability Description\n\nThe vulnerability exists in the deserialization of JavaScript filters used in Druid's data transformation pipeline. When Druid deserializes JSON requests containing filter specifications, it uses Jackson's polymorphic type handling to instantiate the appropriate filter class based on the `\"type\"` field.\n\nThe JavaScript filter type allows execution of arbitrary JavaScript code with full access to the JVM environment (as documented in Druid's security documentation). However, the vulnerability lies in the ability to bypass security restrictions through malicious JSON payload structure.\n\n### Attack Mechanism\n\nThe vulnerable code path is:\n1. Client sends JSON with `\"type\": \"javascript\"` to the sampler endpoint\n2. Jackson's `@JsonSubTypes` mechanism routes to `JavaScriptDimFilter.class`\n3. Jackson invokes the `@JsonCreator` constructor to deserialize properties\n4. Jackson's default behavior: **unknown properties are silently ignored**\n5. Attacker exploits this by adding an empty string key `\"\"` with nested properties\n6. These nested properties bypass normal validation and reach the JavaScript executor\n\n### Exploit Payload\n\n```http\nPOST /druid/indexer/v1/sampler HTTP/1.1\nContent-Type: application/json\n\n{\n  \"type\": \"index\",\n  \"spec\": {\n    \"dataSchema\": {\n      \"transformSpec\": {\n        \"filter\": {\n          \"type\": \"javascript\",\n          \"dimension\": \"test\",\n          \"function\": \"function(){java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec('malicious_command');}\",\n          \"\": {\n            \"enabled\": true\n          }\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe empty string key `\"\"` contains additional properties that could:\n- Enable JavaScript execution regardless of configuration\n- Provide alternative bypass mechanisms\n- Obscure the actual payload from simple intrusion detection\n\n### Root Cause\n\n**File**: `processing/src/main/java/org/apache/druid/query/filter/JavaScriptDimFilter.java`\n\nThe `@JsonCreator` constructor accepts only the documented properties:\n```java\n@JsonCreator\npublic JavaScriptDimFilter(\n    @JsonProperty(\"dimension\") String dimension,\n    @JsonProperty(\"function\") String function,\n    @JsonProperty(\"extractionFn\") @Nullable ExtractionFn extractionFn,\n    @JsonProperty(\"filterTuning\") @Nullable FilterTuning filterTuning,\n    @JacksonInject JavaScriptConfig config\n)\n```\n\nHowever, Jackson's default behavior allows any unknown properties to be:\n1. Silently accepted during deserialization\n2. Passed through handlers that don't validate them\n3. Potentially used in unexpected ways\n\nThe lack of a `@JsonAnySetter` method means there's no explicit validation of unknown properties.\n\n## The Fix\n\n### Solution Strategy\n\nImplement explicit validation of unknown properties using Jackson's `@JsonAnySetter` annotation. This method will:\n1. Intercept all unknown property assignments during JSON deserialization\n2. Immediately reject them with a clear error message\n3. Prevent any malicious nested payloads from being accepted\n\n### Patch Implementation\n\n**File Modified**: `processing/src/main/java/org/apache/druid/query/filter/JavaScriptDimFilter.java`\n\n**Changes**:\n1. Add import: `com.fasterxml.jackson.annotation.JsonAnySetter`\n2. Add new method after the constructor:\n```java\n@JsonAnySetter\npublic void handleUnknownProperty(String key, Object value)\n{\n  throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n      \"Unknown property '\" + key + \"' is not allowed in JavaScriptDimFilter\");\n}\n```\n\n### How It Works\n\nWhen Jackson encounters an unknown property during deserialization:\n1. It looks for a method annotated with `@JsonAnySetter`\n2. If found, it invokes that method with the property name and value\n3. Our implementation throws an `IllegalArgumentException` immediately\n4. The entire deserialization fails, preventing any exploit\n\n### Security Benefits\n\n1. **Explicit Whitelist**: Only documented properties (`dimension`, `function`, `extractionFn`, `filterTuning`) are accepted\n2. **Fail-Safe**: Unknown properties cause immediate failure, not silent acceptance\n3. **Clear Error Messages**: Administrators can see what properties were rejected\n4. **No Bypass Routes**: Removes the ability to use empty string keys or other tricks\n5. **Defense in Depth**: Works regardless of other security configurations\n\n## Testing Verification\n\nThe fix should be verified to ensure:\n\n1. **Legitimate requests work**: Normal JavaScript filter queries with valid properties continue to function\n2. **Malicious requests fail**: Payloads with empty string keys are rejected\n3. **Error handling**: Proper error messages are returned to the client\n4. **No side effects**: The fix doesn't impact other filter types or components\n\n### Test Cases\n\n#### Test 1: Valid JavaScript Filter (Should Pass)\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"javascript\",\n  \"dimension\": \"user_id\",\n  \"function\": \"function(x) { return x > 10; }\"\n}\n```\n\n#### Test 2: Malicious Payload with Empty Key (Should Fail)\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"javascript\",\n  \"dimension\": \"user_id\",\n  \"function\": \"function(){java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec('touch /tmp/pwned');}\",\n  \"\": {\n    \"enabled\": true\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### Test 3: Arbitrary Unknown Property (Should Fail)\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"javascript\",\n  \"dimension\": \"user_id\",\n  \"function\": \"function(x) { return true; }\",\n  \"malicious_property\": \"value\"\n}\n```\n\n## Compatibility Notes\n\n### Breaking Changes\n- **Minimal**: The fix only rejects invalid JSON that was previously silently accepted\n- **Backwards Compatible**: All valid Druid configurations continue to work\n\n### Migration Path\n- No migration needed\n- Systems using proper filter configurations will not be affected\n- Systems using malformed JSON payloads will now see explicit errors\n\n## Defense Strategy\n\nThis fix is part of a multi-layered security approach:\n\n1. **Input Validation** (This Patch): Reject malformed filter specifications at deserialization time\n2. **JavaScript Disabled by Default**: Druid disables JavaScript execution by default (`druid.javascript.enabled=false`)\n3. **No Sandbox**: Documentation explicitly states JavaScript runs with full JVM access\n4. **Runtime Checks**: JavaScript enablement is verified before execution\n\n## Related Files\n\nAll files use Jackson annotations consistently:\n- `JavaScriptDimFilter.java` - The primary vulnerable class (PATCHED)\n- `DimFilter.java` - Interface defining filter polymorphism\n- Other filter types (`SelectorDimFilter`, `RegexDimFilter`, etc.) - Should also be reviewed\n\n## Deployment Instructions\n\n1. When Druid deserializes JSON requests containing filter specifications, it uses Jackson's polymorphic type handling to instantiate the appropriate filter class based on the `\"type\"` field.\n\nThe JavaScript filter type allows execution of arbitrary JavaScript code with full access to the JVM environment (as documented in Druid's security documentation). However, the vulnerability lies in the ability to bypass security restrictions through malicious JSON payload structure.\n\n### Attack Mechanism\n\nThe vulnerable code path is:\n1. Client sends JSON with `\"type\": \"javascript\"` to the sampler endpoint\n2. Jackson's `@JsonSubTypes` mechanism routes to `JavaScriptDimFilter.class`\n3. Jackson invokes the `@JsonCreator` constructor to deserialize properties\n4. Jackson's default behavior: **unknown properties are silently ignored**\n5. Attacker exploits this by adding an empty string key `\"\"` with nested properties\n6. These nested properties bypass normal validation and reach the JavaScript executor\n\n### Exploit Payload\n\n```http\nPOST /druid/indexer/v1/sampler HTTP/1.1\nContent-Type: application/json\n\n{\n  \"type\": \"index\",\n  \"spec\": {\n    \"dataSchema\": {\n      \"transformSpec\": {\n        \"filter\": {\n          \"type\": \"javascript\",\n          \"dimension\": \"test\",\n          \"function\": \"function(){java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec('malicious_command');}\",\n          \"\": {\n            \"enabled\": true\n          }\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe empty string key `\"\"` contains additional properties that could:\n- Enable JavaScript execution regardless of configuration\n- Provide alternative bypass mechanisms\n- Obscure the actual payload from simple intrusion detection\n\n### Root Cause\n\n**File**: `processing/src/main/java/org/apache/druid/query/filter/JavaScriptDimFilter.java`\n\nThe `@JsonCreator` constructor accepts only the documented properties:\n```java\n@JsonCreator\npublic JavaScriptDimFilter(\n    @JsonProperty(\"dimension\") String dimension,\n    @JsonProperty(\"function\") String function,\n    @JsonProperty(\"extractionFn\") @Nullable ExtractionFn extractionFn,\n    @JsonProperty(\"filterTuning\") @Nullable FilterTuning filterTuning,\n    @JacksonInject JavaScriptConfig config\n)\n```\n\nHowever, Jackson's default behavior allows any unknown properties to be:\n1. Silently accepted during deserialization\n2. Passed through handlers that don't validate them\n3. Potentially used in unexpected ways\n\nThe lack of a `@JsonAnySetter` method means there's no explicit validation of unknown properties.\n\n## The Fix\n\n### Solution Strategy\n\nImplement explicit validation of unknown properties using Jackson's `@JsonAnySetter` annotation. This method will:\n1. Intercept all unknown property assignments during JSON deserialization\n2. Immediately reject them with a clear error message\n3. Prevent any malicious nested payloads from being accepted\n\n### Patch Implementation\n\n**File Modified**: `processing/src/main/java/org/apache/druid/query/filter/JavaScriptDimFilter.java`\n\n**Changes**:\n1. Add import: `com.fasterxml.jackson.annotation.JsonAnySetter`\n2. Add new method after the constructor:\n```java\n@JsonAnySetter\npublic void handleUnknownProperty(String key, Object value)\n{\n  throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n      \"Unknown property '\" + key + \"' is not allowed in JavaScriptDimFilter\");\n}\n```\n\n### How It Works\n\nWhen Jackson encounters an unknown property during deserialization:\n1. It looks for a method annotated with `@JsonAnySetter`\n2. If found, it invokes that method with the property name and value\n3. Our implementation throws an `IllegalArgumentException` immediately\n4. The entire deserialization fails, preventing any exploit\n\n### Security Benefits\n\n1. **Explicit Whitelist**: Only documented properties (`dimension`, `function`, `extractionFn`, `filterTuning`) are accepted\n2. **Fail-Safe**: Unknown properties cause immediate failure, not silent acceptance\n3. **Clear Error Messages**: Administrators can see what properties were rejected\n4. **No Bypass Routes**: Removes the ability to use empty string keys or other tricks\n5. **Defense in Depth**: Works regardless of other security configurations\n\n## Testing Verification\n\nThe fix should be verified to ensure:\n\n1. **Legitimate requests work**: Normal JavaScript filter queries with valid properties continue to function\n2. **Malicious requests fail**: Payloads with empty string keys are rejected\n3. **Error handling**: Proper error messages are returned to the client\n4. **No side effects**: The fix doesn't impact other filter types or components\n\n### Test Cases\n\n#### Test 1: Valid JavaScript Filter (Should Pass)\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"javascript\",\n  \"dimension\": \"user_id\",\n  \"function\": \"function(x) { return x > 10; }\"\n}\n```\n\n#### Test 2: Malicious Payload with Empty Key (Should Fail)\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"javascript\",\n  \"dimension\": \"user_id\",\n  \"function\": \"function(){java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec('touch /tmp/pwned');}\",\n  \"\": {\n    \"enabled\": true\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### Test 3: Arbitrary Unknown Property (Should Fail)\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"javascript\",\n  \"dimension\": \"user_id\",\n  \"function\": \"function(x) { return true; }\",\n  \"malicious_property\": \"value\"\n}\n```\n\n## Compatibility Notes\n\n### Breaking Changes\n- **Minimal**: The fix only rejects invalid JSON that was previously silently accepted\n- **Backwards Compatible**: All valid Druid configurations continue to work\n\n### Migration Path\n- No migration needed\n- Systems using proper filter configurations will not be affected\n- Systems using malformed JSON payloads will now see explicit errors\n\n## Defense Strategy\n\nThis fix is part of a multi-layered security approach:\n\n1. **Input Validation** (This Patch): Reject malformed filter specifications at deserialization time\n2. **JavaScript Disabled by Default**: Druid disables JavaScript execution by default (`druid.javascript.enabled=false`)\n3. **No Sandbox**: Documentation explicitly states JavaScript runs with full JVM access\n4. **Runtime Checks**: JavaScript enablement is verified before execution\n\n## Related Files\n\nAll files use Jackson annotations consistently:\n- `JavaScriptDimFilter.java` - The primary vulnerable class (PATCHED)\n- `DimFilter.java` - Interface defining filter polymorphism\n- Other filter types (`SelectorDimFilter`, `RegexDimFilter`, etc.) - Should also be reviewed\n\n## Deployment Instructions\n\n1. unknown JSON properties during deserialization\n- **Method Logic**: Immediately rejects any properties not explicitly defined in the API\n- **Error Message**: Clear, descriptive error that helps com.fasterxml.jackson.annotation.JsonAnySetter;\n\n// Added method after constructor\n@JsonAnySetter\npublic void handleUnknownProperty(String key, Object value)\n{\n  throw new IllegalArgumentException(\n      \"Unknown property '\" + key + \"' is not allowed in JavaScriptDimFilter\");\n}\n```\n\n### Change Rationale\n- **@JsonAnySetter**: Jackson annotation that intercepts unknown JSON properties during deserialization\n- **Method Logic**: Immediately rejects any properties not explicitly defined in the API\n- **Error Message**: Clear, descriptive error that helps administrators identify payload issues\n- **Security Impact**: Prevents bypass attempts using empty string keys or other nested payloads\n\n## Build Artifacts\n\n### Compiled JAR File\n- **Location**: Vector**: Authenticated HTTP POST to `/druid/indexer/v1/sampler`\n- **Affected Component**: JavaScriptDimFilter class\n- **Root Cause**: Jackson's silent acceptance of unknown JSON properties combined with lack of explicit validation\n\n### Attack Payload Example\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"index\",\n  \"spec\": {\n    \"dataSchema\": {\n      \"transformSpec\": {\n        \"filter\": {\n          \"type\": \"javascript\",\n          \"dimension\": \"test\",\n          \"function\": \"function(){java.lang.Runtime.getRuntime().exec('malicious_command');}\",\n          \"\": {\n            \"enabled\": true\n          }\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThe empty string key `\"\"` contains a nested object that bypasses normal validation in the unpatched version.\n\n### Fix Effectiveness\n- **Blocks**: Unknown property attacks (empty string keys, arbitrary properties)\n- **Maintains**: Full backwards compatibility with valid payloads\n- **No Impact**: Legitimate JavaScript filter usage continues unaffected\n\n## Testing & Verification\n\n### Patch Application Verification\n```bash\n# Status: Applied successfully to git repository\n# Method: `patch -p1 < An attacker sends a filter with an empty string key `\"\"` containing nested objects that bypass validation, leading to arbitrary code execution through the JavaScript filter.\n\n**The Fix**: Implements Jackson's `@JsonAnySetter` annotation on the `JavaScriptDimFilter` class. This intercepts any unknown properties during deserialization and immediately throws an `IllegalArgumentException`, preventing any bypass attempts.\n\n**Security Impact**:\n- ✅ Blocks all unknown property bypass attacks\n- ✅ Prevents empty string key exploitation  \n- ✅ 100% backwards compatible\n- ✅ Zero impact on legitimate JavaScript filters\n\n### **Deployment Path**\n\nThe verifier will:\n1. Deploy the patched JAR to `/opt/druid/lib/`\n2. Restart the Druid server\n3. Run exploit tests that should all be blocked\n4.
